# D3 · pass 1 · REGISTRY: the material families after pass 1

| field | value |
|---|---|
| seat | D3 pass-1 agglomeration. It closes pass 1 with each family's status, the floor pass 1 established, the merges, one new family, the owner's ruling questions and the pass-2 plan. It picks no winner |
| model | `claude-opus-5-5`, asserted from own system identity |
| HEAD | `fff6771c`. `git diff --stat v10.0.1 HEAD -- src` touches only `components/configurator/*` and `src/index.ts`, none of the material, so every prototype and critique measured the published 10.0.1 material |
| inputs, read in full | `PORTFOLIO.md`; `harness/HARNESS.md`; `pass-1/W.md`, `X.md`, `SPECS.md`; `pass-1/D3-{A..F}.md`, `D3-{A..F}-proto.md`, `D3-{A..F}-critique.md`. The A and B critiques were handed to this seat as "returned inline", but both files are on disk, and the on-disk text is what is cited |
| instruments | none. This seat re-measures nothing. Every number cites the report that measured it, and that report stays the source of record (P-3) |
| fences | no source edit, no git write; this file is the only write |
| engines | every WebKit number is **Playwright WebKit 26.5**, paint-only: it paints no `backdrop-filter`, has no `navigator.gpu`, and crashes on any `GlassDock` mount. Every real-Safari cell is **UNMEASURED (owner's safaridriver checkbox)** |

Short citations: `A-crit §4.1` is `D3-A-critique.md` §4.1, `B-proto` is `D3-B-proto.md`, `A-res` is the research report `D3-A.md`, `S-n` is a SPECS shared fact, `W §n` is `W.md`, `X §n` is `X.md`, `P §n` is `PORTFOLIO.md`, `H §n` is `HARNESS.md`.

## 0 · In brief

| id | family | status | conv. | reason in one line |
|---|---|---|---:|---|
| D3-A | ground pole | **ADVANCE** (as A′, the null route) | 60% | the one signal-free guarantee that holds in paint in both engines at rest; every green fails on its plant. Chrome reads as a card, and the held dock sheds |
| D3-B | field signal | **BANK** | 40% | the publication is exact (Δ 0.001), cheap and readback-free, but the family paints A's bound on nearly every surface and adds a silent false-publication class |
| D3-C | cascade ink | **ADVANCE** (as C′, fail-safe) | 50% | the exact WCAG solve in shipped CSS is the strongest ink mechanism measured. Its undeclared default and dead solve fail silently; the critic's fail-safe default closes the first |
| D3-D | calibrated ladder | **BANK** (as the calibration layer) | 45% | the solver and frozen corpus are sound and reproducible; the design is the battery inverted, and W-D cannot fail on a consistent hand edit |
| D3-E | self-grounded ink | **RETIRE** | 40% | refuted: a build with no plate passes M-2 560/560, one ring pixel further out reads 1.04-3.03, and the only passing width is the look the owner called sticker lettering |
| D3-F | yielding field | **BANK** | 40% | W-F green and genuine on library substrates; everything else (hostile grounds, photos, prose, the Dialog) needs another family, and two paths mask |
| D3-G | luminosity pole | **OPEN** (minted here) | — | unresearched. From W finding 2 (T8-T8d), W §3.1 (Fluent acrylic), X finding 6 and A-res finding 8 |

Three findings shape pass 2.

1. **Every family that held legibility over any ground did it with α at or near the any-input bound, or did not hold it.**
   - A's chrome sits at 0.89/0.86 (S-16: bound 0.871/0.854).
   - B's unpublished surfaces paint 0.894/0.853 (B-proto §3).
   - C's chrome over the field reads effective opacity 0.85-0.88, equal to its own opaque escape at 0.86 (C-crit §5). Its fail-safe default is the same bound (C-crit §6).
   - D solves 0.61-0.94 in light and 0.75-0.96 in dark (D-proto §2).
   - E never needed the plate (E-crit §3.1). F holds only over library substrates (F-crit §5 item 1).

   The bound is the floor, not a family. What differs between routes is **the knowledge that licenses a surface to go thinner than the bound**: a declaration (C), a publication (B), a yielding field (F), a corpus band (D), or a blend that pins lightness without α (G, new). Every critic's gestalt read of the bound is the same: legible because it stopped being glass (A-crit §5, C-crit §5, D-crit §4).
2. **The veil had readers, and no witness read them.** A `--card` pole turned about 20 `var(--glass-plate-*)` state fills and dims into same-hue no-ops or brightenings (A-crit §4.4, B-crit §4.3-4.4, C-proto break 4, D-proto break 2):
   - active pills vanished in A (first pass), B, C (before its cure), E and F;
   - the focus veil brightens instead of dimming in A, B and D;
   - the grasp register (`grasp.css:133`, 0.6 × rest) sheds legibility under every signal-free ladder that was measured: A dock 1.77/1.61, B dock 2.92-3.28, D wash 1.97/1.95 (35 rows).

   These are D-2 regressions the owner sees before any number. The pass-2 battery reads them (§3.3).
3. **Every family's own witness converged partly by construction.**
   - E's M-2 green is the stroke's token pair (E-crit §3.1).
   - C's dead solve passes M-2 unchanged (C-crit §3).
   - D's W-D leg 1 passes hand edits (D-crit §3).
   - A's W-A leg 1 reads a name (A-crit §3).
   - B's W-B reads a mean the solve never uses (B-crit §3).
   - F's W-F leg 2 is blind to a narrow halo on an isolated surface (F-crit §2).
   - M-5's readback half is vacuous for every family that deletes the sampler.

   In pass 2, a witness counts only if it fails under the plant that removes the route's own mechanism (§7.5).

## 2 · Family entries

### D3-A · The ground pole: ADVANCE as A′, 60%

**Proved by running** (A-proto, re-run exactly by A-crit §2; every green caught by a plant, A-crit §3):

| result | measure |
|---|---|
| no grey | M-1 PASS 11 in Chromium and 10 in WebKit: plates L 0.979-0.984, ΔE 0.005-0.010; the real Dialog `#fef7f1`, L 0.980. Two changes did it: the card lift to `hsl(30 85% 97%)` and the light dialog at α 1 |
| chrome guarantee | every chrome row clears 4.5 over all seven grounds in both themes, in both engines. The 0.02 engine margin in light chrome (0.89) lifted WebKit from 4.44 to 4.61 |
| live dock | 0 readbacks in all 8 windows; dock 5.42-6.83 fg and muted, where HEAD reads 3.51-3.89 in dark |
| dark ladder | step 0.08: adjacent 0.012/0.012/0.029/0.012, page ΔE 0.078-0.146 |
| toolchain | vue-tsc on both projects, 878 style/dock/field/composable tests, library build; `G-BUNDLE-RATCHET` needs a −18,768 B rebind |

**Strongest counterexample.** The chrome guarantee holds only at rest. `grasp.css:133` sheds a held dock to α 0.534/0.516. Held over black in light, the dock reads **1.77**; over white in dark, **1.61**; over the c1 aurora, 3.49/3.67 (A-crit §4.1).

Runner-up: `<Aurora />` at its default `opacityCeiling: 1` under a `<Card>` reads light wash muted 3.38 and dark 3.72. The library's own defaults sit outside the content declaration (A-crit §4.3).

**Open gaps** (source of record: A-crit §8, 14 items):

| kind | gaps | goes to |
|---|---|---|
| route | 1 · the dark `--ink-perimeter` arm (about 0.54, unmeasured; M-3 dark 2.82-2.90 on every ground). 2 · the held dock (above). 5 · the content declaration against the library defaults; blob, constellation and fourier fields unmeasured at c ≤ 0.5. 7 · no engine margin on the content band (WebKit light c0.5 wash 4.35). 10 · `backdropMode: "opaque"` duplicates the level-0 escape, and no consumer sets it. 12 · the gestalt: chrome reads as paper and dark docks as brown pills, with no engagement register | A′ (§7.3) |
| floor | 3 · 15 uncured plate-as-fill readers → FL-6. 4 · the bound as a copied literal in three places, its solver in scratch (an ink retune turns 105 M-2 rows red while the unit gate stays green) → FL-5. 6 · W-A legs 1 and 4 → H-3, H-4. 8 · the M-4 specimen → H-2. 11 · stale prose → FL-12 | §3 |
| owner | 9 · M-6 in light → Q-1. 12 in part → Q-3 | §5 |
| engines | 13 · real Safari, and the WebKit demo gestalt (it crashes on `GlassDock`) | H-14, owner action |
| commit | 14 · the ratchet rebind; the consumer E-6 addenda | landing wave |

**Why it advances.** It is the only family whose guarantee needs nothing to arrive, so E-2 has nothing to mask. It holds in both engines and deletes 746 dead lines. Gaps 1-11 close inside the family or on the floor. Gap 12 is structural to its centre and goes to the owner (§5 Q-3), so A′ runs as the null route: the design that ships if nothing better converges, and the one every other route must beat on transmission and gestalt while holding the same battery.

### D3-B · The field signal: BANK, 40%

**Proved by running** (B-proto; B-crit §2 on a clean raw):

| result | measure |
|---|---|
| publication | published against painted mean under the dock: Δ 0.001 / 0.001 / 0.001 / 0.000 in all four field cells (clean raw) |
| cost | GPU 71-127 µs per frame; `mapAsync` 1.8-2.8 ms p50; 0 main-thread readbacks (S-17) |
| legibility | M-2 560/560 in Chromium and 532/532 in WebKit. Lowest: dark popover muted over c1, 4.61 |
| thinning where published | the dark dock at α 0.44-0.60; the light sidebar over the hero at 0.463; the popover over the pale aurora at 0.34 (B-crit §6) |
| `declareField` on a `<canvas>` | lo/hi 0.039 (navy) against 0.641 (sand); card α 0.860 against 0.273 (B-proto §0.4) |

**Strongest counterexample.** A false publication. A 40 px undeclared band, standing in for a photo or a dark section, sits between the aurora and a published dock. The dock stays `data-field="published"` and nothing fails loud. Dock fg/muted:
- light c1: 3.90/3.90;
- light c0.5: 2.70/2.70;
- dark c1: 3.56/3.52;
- **dark c0.5: 1.46/1.44** (B-crit §4.1).

The bound cannot fail this way. The occluder list (`.bg-overlay-scrim, .glass-focus-veil, [data-glass-occluder]`) is the load-bearing step reduced to three selectors.

**Open gaps** (B-crit §7, 11): false publication (1); the grasp register inverted, held dock 2.92-3.28 at c1 (2); state affordances erased by the value-form collapse, active pill within 1/255 of the plate (3); focus veil sign-wrong, sheet seam doubled (4); W-B aims at the mean, not the lo/hi the solve reads (5); latency not robust, and an unpublished dip on every ceiling change (6); M-1 by 0.001, M-6 adjacency 0.000 in both themes (7); M-3 and M-4 red (8); P-3 costs, including `--card` leaving the `light-dark()` fold and `useAccentTone`'s literal twin (9); coverage: the WebGL2 and `"css"` arms, `useAurora`, blob and constellation publish nothing (10); engines (11).

**Why it is banked.** Where it is honest it is sound, but it is dominated as a whole-material design. It paints A's bound on every unregistered surface, every non-WebGPU arm, every non-substrate page and the c1 light hero (where the solve needs α 0.43-0.87). Its one differentiator, a thinner veil on four registered components over one engine arm, brings a silent unsafe failure (B-crit §6-7).

**Salvage.** `reduceFrame.ts`, as the publication primitive, is banked for D3-BF (§4 M-4). The encoded-tone solve with the unpublished initials lo 0 / hi 1 is taken now as the floor's single source for the bound (FL-5).

### D3-C · The cascade ink: ADVANCE as C′, 50%

**Proved by running** (C-proto; C-crit §2 reproduced every count exactly):

| result | measure |
|---|---|
| the exact solve | muted lands 4.96-5.05 against a 5.0 target over paper in both themes, and again after a consumer retunes `--card` and `--background` (W-C 18/18 in band). It is byte-identical across engines (S-4); `l³ ≈ Y` missed by −16% to +10% |
| two registers | M-4 PASS 14: light tab `#5c493a`, label `#7c6858`, ΔL_OK 0.111; dark 0.088 |
| dark ladder | plates lift 0.051-0.116 off the page and read as warm dark glass (C-crit §5: "the real win") |
| library grounds | M-2 green on paper, c1 and c0.5 in both themes; dark over c1 goes from HEAD's fg 2.12-4.07 to 10.61-13.06 |
| the fail-safe default (critic's plant) | `:root { --glass-ground-lo: #000; --glass-ground-hi: #fff }` gives M-2 560/560, M-3 266/266 (the dark Dialog well included) and M-8 8/8, and closes the portal hole. The cost is thick undeclared glass with a compressed hierarchy (W-C over paper to 11.78) (C-crit §6) |

**Strongest counterexample.** The dead solve. With `--glass-ink-dir: nonsense` the whole solve is invalid at computed time, and every muted glyph inherits `--foreground`. M-2 then reads **124/560, identical to the unplanted run**, and calls it the best legibility in the battery. Only W-C and four M-4 dock rows see it (C-crit §3).

Runner-up: a teleported Popover over a consumer-declared black region reads fg 1.62, muted 2.02, because the declaration seat is keyed on DOM ancestry (C-crit §4).

**Open gaps** (C-crit §8, 16):
1. The fail-safe default is not in the spec.
2. The dead solve fails silently.
3. The publication is one document-global pair, last writer wins, and an unmount clobbers another Aurora's pair; there is no `useAurora` publication.
4. Light chrome over the field is as opaque as the escape (0.85-0.88); the light ladder is bimodal.
5. The light ladder is flat over paper (S-7), and the escape is the most chromatic plate (S-6).
6. The dark ladder is thick (0.32-0.64), with a 0.001 margin at wash, and the dark dock lost its lustre.
7. The dark Dialog well (2.53/2.83).
8. The 20 `--glass-plate-*` sites form a dual path.
9. The ink snaps in one frame on a ground change (D-1).
10. Motion erodes the static bound, about 1 ratio in 100 s.
11. The hand-off selector list misses five scope kinds, and a direct-child scope reads its grandparent's ground.
12. The token-graph gate reads the inherited hand-off as a cycle.
13. The generator is outside the repo, and CSS grows 16-18% raw.
14. `backdropMode: "live"` no longer describes its behaviour.
15. M-5's readback leg is vacuous.
16. Real Safari.

**Why it advances.** Its centre is sound, and with the fail-safe default the shared battery is green except the owner conflicts (C-crit §8). The solve is also separable. If C′ does not converge, the solve is banked as a component, not the family.

### D3-D · The calibrated ladder: BANK as the calibration layer, 45%

**Proved by running** (D-proto; D-crit §2):
- Six of eight M witnesses green in Chromium: M-2, M-3, M-4, M-5, M-7, M-8.
- The solve is deterministic: a second `emit.mjs` wrote a byte-identical file, and α* agrees between Metal and SwiftShader at p95 0.003 (S-19).
- The liveness check refused four dead blob frames and named them.
- The battery reproduced to the row.

**Strongest counterexample.** The gate grades its own author. A fake worktree carries a hand-set floating α of 0.300 and line 1 `/* solver: cp src/styles/tokens/glass.solved.css "$D3_SOLVE_OUT" */`, and W-D leg 1 **passes**: byte-identical. A second plant edits `stages.json` and the committed file together, and it also passes (D-crit §3).

As a design: every dark rung paints below the page (`#030202` down to `#000000` against `#0b0a09`), and M-6 passes because it reads ΔE without a sign (D-crit §5.3).

**Open gaps** (D-crit §6, 16):
1. No dark-lift term.
2. The band ruling is unmade; under the as-written band, light content transmits 0.39 and chrome 0.27.
3. Unsolved states: held (35 rows), `--glass-saturate-grasp` 1.75 hand-set, the collapsed dock.
4. Consumer and hand paints on rungs escape the solve; the rim tile reads 2.21.
5. W-D leg 1 passes hand edits, and leg 2 is unbound to it.
6. Stage 2 is a hand constant (0.82) that breaks PROPORTION.
7. The dialog α is analytic, with a silent `?? 1` clamp.
8. Engine margin (WebKit 4.45-4.48).
9. S-7.
10. The solver imports a tranche doc, `closure.mjs` is absent, and the corpus is 71 MB.
11. P-3 duplicates.
12. Muted sits on the M-4 floor.
13. Floor row 5 not done.
14. The G2 dynamic-darken edict is retired, not met.
15. Wash's r 0 is misreported as a solve choice.
16. Residue.

**Why it is banked.** The machinery converged and the design did not. Its objective is the battery inverted, so it fails exactly where the battery is silent: sign, transmission, held states, consumer paint. The solver is the strongest instrument any family has for setting a ladder's numbers, and it outlives the family (§4 M-5).

### D3-E · The self-grounded ink: RETIRE, 40%

**Refutation** (E-crit §3, §7), on three counts:
1. **Its green is the instrument's geometry.** With no plate at all (`--glass-veil-base: 0; --glass-veil-step: 0`), M-2 passes 560/560 and M-3 266/266. W-E improves, 2 red rows against the prototype's 4. The stroke was sized to the harness ring (E-res §0.1: "a centred stroke needs 5 px"). One pixel further out (ring R 2) the same captures read 1.25-3.03 on every non-trivial ground.
2. **Its only passing width is the look the owner rejected.** At 5 px the ground reads as sticker lettering in light and as embossed meme type in dark (E-proto B-2, E-crit §3.7). Between 1 and 2 px it reads worse than no stroke, 1.05-1.14 over black (E-res §0.2). Halos are not portable across engines (E-res §0.3).
3. **Inheritance has an open-ended reset list, and one entry cannot exist.** Neither engine honours a stroke property inside `::selection` (E-proto §2.2). 33 text runs per theme on opaque nested hosts, plus 29 inputs, still carry the card stroke (E-crit §3.4).

**A note on WCAG.** A 5 px halo counts as the glyph's background under WCAG's own border rule (W §5), so the retirement does not rest on a WCAG reading. It rests on the gestalt, and on the material carrying no legibility role, which a material family cannot concede.

**Not BLOCK:** no missing primitive would change the outcome. **Not BANK:** the research measured every width from 1 to 6 px and every halo stack.

**Harvest** (E-crit §7), banked in §3.4:
- `saturate(0.6)` at blur ≥ 16 px, the thin-veil route to M-8's chroma half (S-15);
- the opaque `--perimeter-ink` with a card band, one of the three indicator registers (§5 Q-6);
- the 4× `drop-shadow` icon dilation (4.27/4.37 in Chromium, 3.81/3.74 in WebKit), resolved at the element with nothing mounted.

The `--card` frost is D3-A's move, and the duplicate `--neutral-5` declaration goes to FL-11.

### D3-F · The yielding field: BANK, 40%

**Proved by running** (F-proto; F-crit §1-2):
- **W-F:** 152/152. Light c1 L p05 0.893-0.936; dark c1 p95 0.306-0.362; 24 px outside, ΔE p95 0.0000. The GL arm yields identically in WebKit.
- **M-2 over the aurora:** light c1 muted goes from HEAD's 1.47 to 4.64.
- **Cost:** GPU +0.23-0.32 ms at 4 Mpx for 16 rects.
- **Coverage:** the well is one function in both shading languages, spliced into the aurora's palette and image pipelines and into the blob.
- A dead well, a readback and a spill are each caught by a plant.

**Strongest counterexample.** CE-4: the bound kills the aurora for the session. At 17 registered surfaces, `data-aurora-settled` drops and the canvas falls to its placeholder. Remove two, wait 4 s, and the live field never returns, while the placeholder paints with no well under any glass. It is loud only in the console; in paint it is a plausible field with legibility gone, the masking E-2 forbids (F-crit §3.6). A card list scrolling over a fixed aurora reaches it.

Runner-up: CE-2 and CE-5. Glass at `opacity: 0`, or clipped away by an `overflow: hidden` ancestor, still carves a pale (light) or dark (dark) rectangle at full depth (ΔE p50 0.228-0.389).

**Open gaps** (F-crit §5, 13):
1. Non-substrate grounds (black, white, checker, stripes, prose, photos, canvases).
2. The light Dialog (S-8).
3. The 16-rect bound and its unwelled fall.
4. Registration coverage (`.glass-capsule`, `.control-surface`, `.configurator-shell`, `.input-bar`).
5. Paint-blind geometry.
6. The well has no motion of its own (D-1).
7. A stale well on parked substrates (WAAPI).
8. Unwelled arms fail silently.
9. The gestalt: dark plates read as holes, the light-paper structure vanishes, and light active pills are lost.
10. Dual paths and stale prose.
11. M-3, M-4, M-8 and floor row 5 untouched.
12. W-F's blind spots.
13. Real Safari's backdrop edge, which could force a 20-44 px halo.

**Why it is banked.** It is real in paint on its own scope, but half the problem, legibility over grounds the library does not paint, sits outside its charter. Its M-1 and dark M-6 greens are D3-A's pole swap (F-crit §3.1). It reopens only as a substrate-side complement to whichever plate family wins.

## 3 · The shared floor

### 3.1 · Facts pass 1 established

S-1..S-25 (SPECS "Shared facts") remain the floor of record, and so do the consumer constraints of `X.md` §3.4. Pass 1's prototypes and critiques added the rows below.

**Settled by several prototypes**
- **PF-1 · The sampler is dead weight in every family.** All six deleted the 746 lines, `backgroundCanvas`, the clamp, the knee, `--glass-backdrop-luma` and its `@property`, and the `--glass-backdrop` bucket. All six read M-5 green with 0 readbacks, and each critic caught a planted readback (A-crit, B-crit, E-crit, F-crit §2).
- **PF-2 · `contain: paint` goes.** M-7 is green in all six, and each critic's restored `contain: paint` reds it 8/8. The removal does not free grasp carriers: the rung's own `backdrop-filter` is the backdrop root (S-2). The prose at `material.css:97-103` names the wrong cause.
- **PF-3 · Every cure of the light grey was the `--card` pole.** A, B, C, E and F each read M-1 plates within ΔE 0.002-0.016 of paper on it. E's and F's M-1 greens are this move, not their mechanisms (E-crit §7, F-crit §3.1). D's solver chose a white pole (`rgb(255 255 251)`, C 0.0053), which reads as neutral white rather than cream (D-crit §4).
- **PF-4 · The unsignalled state is the any-input bound.** Three families reached it independently:
  - A's chrome bound (0.871/0.854 analytic; painted 0.865/0.855);
  - B's unpublished state (painted 0.898/0.859);
  - C's fail-safe default (C-crit §6).

  It is the only default measured E-2-clean. A page-publishes-its-ground default paints thin and wrong over anything undeclared (B-res §0.4: light fg 1.01 over black). So does C's page-ground default (M-2 124/560).
- **PF-5 · Every route that cured the real Dialog made it a room.** HEAD's Dialog transmits its scrim (S-8). The cures that read M-1 green on the real Dialog each bring its plate near α 1 over the scrimmed ground: A's light α 1 (L 0.980), C's room floor (L 0.981), D's scrim-aware 0.937 (L 0.984). B's bound over the scrim reads L 0.956. E's and F's thin plates stay grey (L 0.758, 0.729).
- **PF-6 · The dock's private re-compositions go.** A, C and D dropped the light `saturate(1.2)` and the dark `saturate(1.3) brightness(1.14)`. E kept them, and its dock info row reads bleed 1.51-1.63 and C 0.071-0.090.
- **PF-7 · The Dialog Close ring routes through the perimeter token** (S-12). Routed, it reads 3.25-3.32 light under A, 8.37-8.88 under D and 4.66-5.10 under E, against HEAD's 1.80.

**Refuted or constrained mechanisms**
- **PF-8 · The grasp register sheds legibility on every signal-free ladder.** `calc(var(--glass-veil-rest) * 0.6)` measured:
  - A dock 1.77 (light, over black) and 1.61 (dark, over white);
  - B dock 2.92-3.28 over c1;
  - D wash 1.97/1.95, 35 M-2 rows.

  C's probe had made the rung invalid, so it did nothing until C-proto restored it; C's held legibility is unmeasured.
- **PF-9 · A `--card` pole inverts every plate-as-fill reader.** Consumers of `--glass-plate-*` or `--glass-veil-step` that paint state or a dim turn into same-hue no-ops or brightenings:
  - hover and active pills (A §4.4, B §4.3, C break 3-4, E B-4, F §5);
  - the focus veil, which brightens instead of dimming (A-proto §4; B-proto §0.6: `rgb(133 119 113)` → `rgb(182 170 163)`; D-proto §4);
  - the doubled sheet seam (B-crit §4.4).

  A and C cured the dock fills by routing them to the ink fill register (A: `--fill-hover`/`--fill-selected`; C: `--foreground` at one and two steps).
- **PF-10 · A default that equals the page is a masking fallback** (C-crit §4, B-res §0.4). So is a dead thin arm that paints plausibly: C's dead solve, F's unwelled placeholder, B's false publication.
- **PF-11 · A static publication is exact only at t = 0.** C's ink snaps in one frame on a ceiling step. Over 100 s of the moving field, dark card muted went from 5.98 frozen to 5.01 moving (C-crit §5). B dips unpublished on every ceiling change (B-crit §4.5). F's well is binary per rect and trails by one frame (F-crit §4 CE-6).

**Instrument facts**
- **PF-12 · The harness `raw` leak (S-10) is larger than SPECS measured.** Under B's prototype it reads −0.055 in light c1 (B-proto §0.3). Only B's critic cured it, and only in a copy.
- **PF-13 · M-5's fed window is the default window** once `backgroundCanvas` is deleted, which every family does.
- **PF-14 · The muted ink is declared twice**, at `color-radius.css:45` and `light-dark.css:98`, and the second wins. This predates D3 (E-crit §2, B3).
- **PF-15 · Engine margin.** WebKit's glyph raster costs 0.13-0.20 of ratio at a solved bound: A 4.44 against 4.65, D 4.45-4.48 against 4.65. D's 0.15 margin was short by 0.02-0.05, and A's 0.02 α margin held (4.61).

## 4 · Merges: one mechanism, one family

| id | merged | why | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-1 | A's chrome bound, B's unpublished arm, C's fail-safe default | the same guarantee (any-input α on the card pole), reached three times and measured within 0.02 α | → FL-4 and FL-5. No route owns the bound |
| M-2 | A's content band into C's ground declaration | the same idea: a declared ground licenses thinner content glass. A's form is prose and refuted by the library's own `<Aurora />` default (A-crit §4.3); C's form is enforceable in the cascade (`@utility glass-ground`) | A′ drops its prose band (§5 Q-2 option a). Declared thinning belongs to C′ alone |
| M-3 | E's and F's grey cures into A's pole | both cured M-1 by the `--card` swap, not by their mechanisms | → FL-3 |
| M-4 | B's and F's surface registries | both register glass over a substrate, and both fail on the same missing piece: knowing that nothing is painted between the substrate and the glass (B's three-selector occluder list, F's paint-blind geometry and selector coverage). The information flows in opposite directions (the substrate publishes, against the field yields), but the dependency is one primitive | if either re-trigger fires, both reopen as **D3-BF, the substrate-coupled material**, with two arms (publish α, yield field) on one registry and one claim rule. A selector list does not qualify as that rule (B-crit §7) |
| M-5 | D's solver into the instrument | the solver's constraints are the battery's thresholds restated (D-crit §4). It is a calibration method, not a material | banked as the calibration layer; D's emitted design (white/black poles, the as-written band) is dominated |

**Considered and not minted, after pass 1.**
- D3-X1 (tone-map frost) stays merged into A.
- D3-X2 (the surface samples) is confirmed dead (S-13).
- D3-X3 (`plus-darker` vibrancy ink) stays unminted: Chromium 149 lacks `plus-darker`.
- D3-X4 (`contrast-color()`) is confirmed binary and alpha-blind (S-3). Color 6's target form is "Not Ready For Implementation" (W §6.2).
- D3-X5 (declared role split) returns as C's enforceable declaration (M-2).
- D3-X6 (two authored ladders) stays HEAD's disease.

D3-G (§6) is neither X1 (an affine filter chain) nor X3 (a blend that sets the ink): it uses the non-separable `luminosity` blend, which both engines ship, as the plate.

## 5 · Ruling questions for the owner

Each row is a question that pass 1 cannot settle from measurement. The options are the ones the prototypes built or named, and the evidence cites the report of record. Pass 2 builds on the default column until the owner rules. A route that finds a default unbuildable reports it with measurements rather than working around it.

| id | question | options | evidence | pass-2 default |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-1 | **Light ladder separation.** M-1 and M-6 cannot both hold in light for any pole and any α | (a) strike M-6's light adjacency; light levels separate by optics (blur, rim, shadow, elevation) under a new witness. (b) widen M-1's radius so paint can step, which readmits a tint (HEAD's grey sat at ΔE 0.039-0.142). (c) collapse the light paint ladder to three materials: content, chrome, room | S-7 (proof D-res §0.3); all six families measured light adjacency 0.000-0.003; D emits INFEASIBLE | (a); H2 writes the optics witness if the owner confirms |
| Q-2 | **What content glass guarantees muted AA over** (the band ruling, D's largest lever) | (a) any input: every rung at or above the bound, a frosted card everywhere. (b) a declared ground, enforced in the cascade, fail-safe to the bound. (c) library fields at ceiling ≤ 0.5 by prose, true only if `Aurora` and `Constellation` default to 0.5 (an aurora-owner interface). (d) muted text is not seated on content glass over a field (Apple: "don't use Liquid Glass in the content layer", W §2.1). (e) lightness pinned by a luminosity pole (G, unmeasured) | A-crit §4.3 (3.38/3.72 at the default ceiling); D: light content α 0.600 as-written against 0.113 banded; C-crit §6 (fail-safe); X §3.4 #5 (value.js's user-seeded field needs 0.845) | each route runs its own arm: A′ (a), C′ (b), G (e). The owner rules after pass 2 reports T per rung (H-12) |
| Q-3 | **Chrome: a card or glass.** W-FROST's law says legibility is "never bought with alpha", but every chrome that holds any input reads as a card, and the dock no longer responds to its backdrop, which retires the G2 "darken dynamically like iOS 27" edict | (a) all chrome (floating, overlay, dialog, dock) is a frosted card at the bound; strike the alpha clause for chrome. (b) the rooms (floating, overlay, dialog) are cards, as the consumers ask (UIA-F-122, UIA-KF-252, -268, UIA-V-297), and the dock stays glass that carries the field. (c) all chrome transmits | A-crit §5, C-crit §5 (effective opacity 0.85-0.88), D-proto §2 (T 0.27 chrome, 0.195 dock); D-proto break 12; Apple: Regular "provides legibility regardless of context", the dock is the tab-bar case (W §2.3) | (a) for A′ and C′; G tests (b) |
| Q-4 | **The dark lift.** Which way dark plates leave the page | (a) lift above the page toward the dark card (L 0.295): legible and separable, but brown over saturated red (UIA-V-198). (b) sink below it (D's black pole, the transmission optimum): the ladder reads as holes. (c) a neutral lifted pole, trading warmth for hue fidelity | A-res §0.8 (chrome over `#761617` shifts to hue 34-40, C 0.033-0.048); A-crit §5 (brown docks); D-crit §5.3; A-res §0.8 (the deep pole restores HEAD's one-colour ladder) | (a), with M-6 signed (H-9) |
| Q-5 | **The dark field** (F-24). The default aurora is theme-blind, so dark glass sits over a bright field | (a) the aurora wave re-tones the dark field (F's `--field-well-ambient` is the same function), and dark glass can thin. (b) dark glass carries legibility over the bright field alone | P §2.1; F-res §3 (ambient 1: dark field L 0.301-0.362); the aurora is another wave's (F-24, F-75) | (b). D3 does not assume another wave; F's re-trigger watches (a) |
| Q-6 | **The indicator register against PROPORTION §1.2** (seam : edge : perimeter = 1 : 2 : 6) | (a) a translucent `--ink-perimeter` per theme that keeps 1 : 2 : 6 (A: light 0.52 green on chrome; dark about 0.54, unmeasured). (b) a solved perimeter (C at target 3.6; D at 0.82, which is 1 : 2 : 10.25 and draws near-black outlines). (c) an opaque two-tone band (E: 266/266 in both engines, but focus replaces a glass Button's rim, E-proto B-3) | A-proto §0.4; D-proto break 1, D-crit §4 ("sticker edge"); E-proto §1, B-3 | (a); PROPORTION holds unless the owner rules |
| Q-7 | **E-2's reading of the bound under a thinning arm.** When a declaration, publication or blend is absent, the surface paints the bound, legibly | (a) the bound is the primary guarantee and the thin arm an enhancement; a stamped state attribute plus a witness suffices. (b) a dead thin arm must also fail loud (console or `rendererStatus`, as F's overflow does). (c) one mechanism only; no enhancement arms | B-crit §5 ("a dead primary is silent; a false primary is worse"); C-crit §6 (fail-safe is the E-2 direction); W §9.2 (G's isolated placement goes flat) | (a) and (b) together. - **The safaridriver checkbox.** Every Safari cell in every family is UNMEASURED, including:
  - B's `mapAsync` and `TEXTURE_BINDING` alpha;
  - C's four-level Dialog RCS chain;
  - F's backdrop edge;
  - G's blend with a sibling `backdrop-filter`;

## 6 · New family: D3-G · the luminosity pole (hue through, lightness pinned)

**Source.** W §1 finding 2 and §9.2 (probes T8-T8d), Fluent acrylic's luminosity layer (W §3.1), X §0 finding 6 (consumer isolation), A-res §0.8 (the dark card turns red fields brown). No family adopted it (S-21).

**Charter.** A glass surface composes a luminosity layer beneath its content: a pole colour with `mix-blend-mode: luminosity` over the blurred backdrop. The composite takes the pole's luminosity and the backdrop's hue and saturation. So:
- the ink's contrast is fixed by the pole's luminosity on any ground, with no α bound, no declaration and no signal;
- the field's colour carries through the glass, up to the gamut ceiling at the pinned lightness (P §2.3).

Rungs differ by pole luminosity and blur. The rooms (floating, overlay, dialog), where the consumers ask for calm (M-8), stay at the bound (FL-4, FL-8). The content rungs and the dock take the pole (§5 Q-3 option b).

**Centre:** hue-carrying glass with a signal-free legibility floor. **What it would buy over A′:**
- in dark, a plate lifted to L 0.30-0.35 that keeps a red field red where A's card turns it brown (Q-4);
- a dock that follows the field's colour live with no signal, which speaks to D-2 and the retired G2 edict;
- luminance bleed decoupled from blur, which frees F48's subtle radii.

**Measured so far** (W T8b, a standalone page, not the library):
- **Light:** a pole of `rgb(235 232 228)` lands composites at L 0.927-0.948 over orange, ochre, violet, green, black, white and the stripes. Light muted reads 5.14-5.73, and C runs 0.037-0.092 (median).
- **Dark:** a pole of `rgb(52 44 38)` gives L 0.30-0.40 and dark muted 5.29-7.06.
- WebKit agrees within 0.07.

**Failure mode.** The pole goes flat when the plate or an ancestor between plate and field carries `transform`, `isolation`, `z-index`, `opacity < 1`, `filter` or `will-change`, or when the pole is a child of the `backdrop-filter` element. It also goes flat when the ground is a root-propagated background.

Flat means the pole's own colour. On a card-luminosity pole that is A's frosted card: legible, with no hue, and silent (W §9.2). So the failure degrades to the floor's look, not to illegibility. Whether that is E-2-clean is §5 Q-7.

**Known risks, stated before research.**
1. **M-1 caps the light pole.** Over paper the composite takes the pole's luminosity with paper's near-zero saturation, so a light pole under L 0.975 paints exactly O-62's grey. At L ≥ 0.975 the gamut leaves little chroma for red-orange hues. Analytically that is small, but it is unmeasured. The light arm may therefore collapse to A′, and G would be a dark and dock mechanism.
2. **Isolation is the common case.** Portalled overlays carry `z-index` and floating-ui transforms. D2's advancing routes move seats and possibly the plate by transform (D2 REGISTRY §7.1, D2-G). fourier's app root is `isolate`, and value.js's ground is root-propagated (X §0.6), so value.js would get flat poles everywhere without an addendum.
3. **D2's floor row 6** makes `.dock-plate` the one element that composes the plate and carries the dock's one `backdrop-filter`. The pole must be its sibling, not its child, which is a D2 interface.
4. **The Lum formula** (`0.3R + 0.59G + 0.11B` on encoded channels) is not WCAG Y. L spreads by about 0.02 across hues, so a margin is owed.

**Pass-2 questions, in order.** A BLOCK or RETIRE is declared at the first named failure.
1. **Isolation census.** Which library glass surfaces, at HEAD and under D2's advancing routes, have an isolated group between plate and field? Can the pole sit as a sibling of `.dock-plate` and of portalled overlay content? If the dock and content rungs cannot host a sibling pole without isolation, **BLOCK** on the missing primitive: a blend that reads through an isolated group.
2. **Light under M-1.** At a pole L ≥ 0.975, measure the chroma carried over c1 and c0.5 per hue, against A′'s composites on the same cells. If it matches within ΔC 0.01, G's light arm is dominated: say so, and scope G to dark and the dock.
3. **Dark.** A pole L of 0.28-0.36 against dark ink (≤ 0.41); M-6 signed (H-9); hue fidelity over UIA-V-198's `#761617`.
4. **Engines.** The pole with a sibling `backdrop-filter` in Chromium; WebKit paint-only; real Safari once enabled.
5. **Fail loud.** Can the library detect an isolated placement at mount or on change and stamp it, so the flat state is visible to a witness? Measure the flat paint against FL-4's bound.
6. **Chrome calm.** The dock's M-8 info row with the pole; `saturate(0.6)` from §3.4 if needed.
7. **Cost.** One extra layer per surface: paint and compositing at the demo's largest route.
8. **Consumers.** fourier's `isolate` root and value.js's root-propagated gradient: the addendum each would need (E-6).

**Born-RED witness W-G · hue through, lightness pinned.**
- Over the c1 aurora and over UIA-V-198's red, every content rung and the dock keeps its composite hue within 10° of its raw ground's hue, with OKLCh chroma ≥ 0.5 × the sRGB gamut maximum at its own L and hue, while M-2 holds on the same cells in both themes.
- A plate placed inside an isolated group either keeps that, or carries a stamped state that the witness reads. A silent flat plate is a FAIL.

**HEAD reading:** RED. Over c1, HEAD's composites keep the field's hue (C 0.150-0.220), but muted reads 1.47-1.96 (P §1.1). A's dark chrome over `#761617` holds M-2 but shifts to hue 34-40 at C 0.033-0.048 (A-res §0.8).

## 7 · Pass-2 plan

### 7.1 · Routes

| route | status | centre | competes on |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A′** · the bound, nothing added | ADVANCE (the null route) | static ink, α at the bound on every rung (Q-2 a, Q-3 a) | the baseline every other route must beat on T (H-12) and gestalt (H-13) while holding the same battery |
| **C′** · the cascade ink, fail-safe | ADVANCE | undeclared → the bound; declared → a thin veil, a solved floor and the exact ink solve | transmission over declared library grounds |
| **D3-G** · the luminosity pole | OPEN | lightness pinned by a blend; hue carried | hue fidelity on content rungs and the dock, with the rooms at the bound |

All three build on the floor (§3.2) and the revised battery (§3.3). They differ on Q-2 and Q-3, which is where pass 1 left the problem open.

**Banked and not run:** B and F (as D3-BF, §4 M-4) and D (the calibration layer), each with the re-trigger in §1.1. **Retired:** E, with its harvest in §3.4.

### 7.2 · Order

1. **Floor.** H2 lands first with its self-test (fixture PASS and planted FAIL, both engines), and HEAD is re-run under H2 as the new baseline. Then the floor patch (FL-1..FL-13) lands once, and every route branches from it.
2. **G's questions 1 and 2** run before G builds anything else. A BLOCK at question 1, or domination at question 2, saves the route's cost or narrows its scope.
3. **Routes in parallel.** Each gets research, spec, prototype and critique, one seat per stage. Harness and browser runs are serialized across seats: concurrent browser seats disturb each other, and the machine ran at load 16-225 through pass 1.
4. **Agglomeration** reads the three critiques and rules the next step. The owner's answers to §5 enter at whatever point they arrive.

### 7.3 · What each route must answer and prove

**A′** (from A-crit §8):
1. Content at the bound on every rung. Report T per rung (H-12) and the owner-glance pair (H-13). State what, besides paint, makes a bound-thick plate read as glass (rim, specular, blur, elevation) and as engaged (M-9 state registers).
2. The held dock and every grasp host at or above the bound under FL-7, green on the H-5 cells.
3. The dark perimeter arm, measured under Q-6's default, with M-3 dark green on every ground the band claims.
4. Delete `backdropMode: "opaque"` (the level-0 escape covers it) or name a consumer (E-1).
5. An engine margin on every band: WebKit ≥ 4.5 wherever Chromium claims green.
6. Q-4's dark pole: UIA-V-198's red read, and M-6 signed.
7. value.js's X-W7L outcome (THE STOP) and chicago's per-theme addendum, stated.

**C′** (from C-crit §8):
1. The fail-safe default in the spec, re-measured on H2 (portal cell included).
2. The dead solve fails loud: H-7 green, and the mechanism-removal plant reds M-2 or H-7.
3. Publication per scope, not document-global: two substrates, the unmount clobber, `useAurora`'s WebGL2 and `"css"` arms. Publish the bound, not the mean.
4. **Its differentiator:** T per rung over declared library grounds against A′'s at an equal battery. If there is no gain, C downgrades to BANK and the solve is banked as a component (the critic's trigger).
5. Light chrome over the field under Q-3's default, and the dark ladder's 0.001 wash margin.
6. Q-8's asymmetric inertia on a ground change, H-11 green.
7. Hand-off coverage: the five missing scope kinds, and no grandparent read.
8. The generator in the repo, or the chain hand-written (P-3). CSS growth and recalc reported, Safari when enabled.
9. `backdropMode: "live"` renamed or removed (E-1); the token-graph gate under Q-10.

**D3-G:** §6 questions 1-8 in order, with a BLOCK or RETIRE at each named failure; W-G born-RED on HEAD, with its self-test (H-15); the revised M battery; the owner-glance pair.

**Every route:**
- the floor rows built;
- every cell in both engines under H2;
- WebKit dock rows under H-14's `+shim`;
- a mechanism-removal plant for every green it claims;
- a prose sweep of every touched file;
- real Safari cells as soon as the checkbox is ticked.

### 7.4 · Cross-pollination

**Earned in pass 1**, and all of it goes into the floor rather than from one route into another:
- **A, B, C → FL-4:** the bound is the default state (M-1).
- **B → FL-5:** the bound computed in CSS from tokens. This closes A's source-of-record gap with a mechanism B measured.
- **A, C → FL-6:** state fills read the ink fill register (both cured the dock that way).
- **E → §3.4:** chroma calm by `saturate(0.6)`, the icon dilation, and the two-tone indicator as Q-6 (c).

**Not earned yet.**
- C's exact solve does not move into A′ or G. A′'s centre is static ink, and G must first show its blend holds.
- B's bus and F's well move into no route; they stay banked as D3-BF.
- G's pole does not move into A′'s dock until G has passed its questions 1-2.

A combination such as A′'s rooms with G's dock is the next agglomeration's to propose, on pass-2 measurements.

### 7.5 · Convergence criterion

A route converges when its critic finds zero open gaps against a finite checklist fixed now:
- M-1..M-8 as revised by H-1..H-14, plus M-9 and the route's own witness, in both themes;
- Chromium gated, WebKit informational, Safari when enabled;
- floor rows FL-1..FL-13;
- the route's §7.3 items;
- the owner-glance gestalt read against D-1, D-2 and D-3.

**A green counts only if the route's mechanism-removal plant turns it red.** That plant is E-crit's "no plate" counterexample, generalized: remove the declaration, the solve, the pole or the α and re-run. Critics may not add rows mid-pass; a new defect class is recorded for pass 3's checklist.

No winner is declared until all three routes are measured under the same H2. Convergence is not contemplated before pass 3 (the charter's floor of three passes).

## Owner ruling O-87 (2026-10-06, in-session): a deft refinement and de-slopification

The owner, verbatim: "ios27 everywhere, but a bit flatter, more refined, deft, and slightly less blur." Then: "This is a deft refinement and de-slopification." Source letter: `docs/tranches/BK/coordination/valuejs-outbound-2026-10-06-flat-lighting.md`; row O-87 in `docs/tranches/BL/audit/INBOUND.md`.

This binds every pass from now on, on top of this registry's checklist:
- The iOS-27 liquid-glass canon still governs. This is not a flat redesign.
- Flatter and more refined: strip affectation. That means stacked shadows on chrome, glow halos, coloured drop-glow, gleam or specular beyond one quiet edge, inner top-light bevels that read as decoration, and idle shimmer, pulse or breathe that carries no state.
- Keep the material, its single edge, its motion and its engagement (D-1..D-3). An effect earns its place by carrying state or depth, never by ornament.
- Slightly less blur than the current tokens.
- Judge each route's paint with frames against this ruling. A route that adds lighting layers fails it.

Landing: the token-level refinement rides 10.2.0, as a band-0 token wave. Deeper changes ride this loop.

Three findings shape pass 3.

1. **The light veil at the bound reads grey off paper, and M-1 cannot see it.** Over paper the floor's light plate is `#fef7f2` (ΔE 0.010 from paper). Over black it is `#e2dcd7`, L 0.898, **ΔE 0.027 from HEAD's rejected grey `#d7d4ce`**. Over the glass-material story's deep canvas it is `#e3e3e3`, C 0.000 (Ap-crit §3.3). The owner's frame for this pass is "the light veil must not read grey". The only light composite in pass 2 that carries colour instead of grey is C′'s claimed content over the field (`#ff8266`, salmon, muted 5.53; Cp-proto §6). Chrome stays at the bound in every route.
2. **The bound's thickness is set by the lightest ink a plate is promised to, and pass 2 measured the lever.** FL-5 solves at the static muted ink (light L 0.481): α 0.891. Solved at the `-strong` register: 0.769 any-input, 0.599 over c1. Solved at C′'s reach ink (L 0.306): 0.624 any-input, 0.349 over c1 (Cp-spec §3.3, Cp-crit §4.1). A darker muted ink buys transmission and compresses the hierarchy. That trade is the owner's (Q-12, P2-5).
3. **Two of the three routes ran into the same wall from opposite sides: knowing what is painted between the substrate and the glass.**
   - G cannot see past an isolated group (G-gate §2.5).
   - C′ cannot see library or consumer paint inside a claim: `--secondary` under a `"page"` claim reads muted 4.18 (Cp-crit §6.4); a false field claim reads 1.00 (Cp-proto §4).
   - G-gate §2.5 names a pole that tracks the plate from outside the group as D3-BF's missing claim rule in another form.

   Pass 3 does not open D3-BF (P2-1.4). It records that every thinning arm measured so far rests on a declaration the library cannot verify.

### P2-1.3 · D3-G · BLOCK at question 1

**Measured** (G-gate §2-§3; every Chromium cell and both engines' census taken twice at different load, byte-identical):
- **Isolation census.** `.dock-plate` sits under `.glass-dock` (`z-index: 0`, `contain: layout style`), and the plate's `z-index: −1` needs that group. Portalled overlays sit under the floating wrapper (`fixed`, `z-index: 130`, `transform`), and the Dialog under its own `fixed`, `z-index: 140`. Select, menus, Tooltip, Toast, Sheet, ExpandableContainer and `.card-header--shrink` are the same in source. D2-G's doors flatten the pole in both engines. D2-B′ keeps the dock's group.
- **The pole inside any such group paints the plant**, byte-identical to `mix-blend-mode: normal`: light card C 0.011 h 63.4, dark L 0.295 C 0.022. A `backdrop-filter` sibling inside the group changes nothing (`G-iso` = `P-iso-noblur`).
- **Where nothing isolates, the blend works.** Dark content rungs carry the field: Δh 0.2-3.9°, C 0.078-0.169. Over `#761617` they read L 0.413, C 0.169, Δh 3.0°, against A′'s C 0.029-0.043 at Δh 9-17°. But the rung must give its `backdrop-filter` to a sibling layer (`G-child` is flat), and lightness is not pinned in OKLab: L 0.305-0.413 off a 0.295 pole.
- **Question 2.** At L ≥ 0.975 the composite sits on the gamut ceiling (C/Cmax 1.000). It carries less chroma than A′'s bound rung on every tile in both engines. Against A′'s resting rung it gains at most +0.011 (n975 pole, c0.5, 24-25 of 80 tiles); on the card pole ≤ +0.007.

**Open rows:** the charter's dock and overlay arm is unbuildable. Questions 3-8 were not run.

**Vacuous greens:** none claimed. The flat state is unstamped (`grep glass-flat` finds nothing in `src` or `floor.patch`), so a flat pole would be a silent green-looking card. That goes on record for Q-7.

**Re-trigger** (G-gate §5): either engine ships a blend whose backdrop is the backdrop root's image, **or** the owner rules a dark-content-rung-only family. That is a different charter and would need its own pass (Q-13, P2-5).

### P2-1.4 · Banked and retired, re-checked

## P2-4 · Why no winner, and what the 10.2.0 veil recut can ship from

**No winner.** G was never measured under H2's battery, and §7.5 requires all routes measured under the same H2. The charter also requires a floor of three passes.

**W-0.1 (O-62 + O-66 §1) and its entry gate.** W-0.1 lands only from D3's converged specification. That means at least three full passes, zero enumerated open gaps, a fresh adversarial audit, and **two consecutive clean passes** (`waves/W-0.1.md` §3). Pass 2 is not clean: A′ has 3 open rows, C′ has 9. So:

- **When.** No earlier than the close of pass 4, and only if pass 3 and pass 4 both close clean. Pass 3 can be clean only if the owner rules Q-1 and Q-11 (below) before pass 3's critics run, because A′'s M-6 and its O-62 gestalt rows cannot close by any seat. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88, `FORMATION-PROGRESS.md:112`) and on W-0.11 (O-87), which is D3's as well.
- **What it can ship from.** Only the **light arm of floor v2** (the null route) fits W-0.1's minor-lawful scope:
  - the card pole lift `#fef7f1`;
  - the light rungs at the computed bound (0.891 + whole steps) and the light Dialog at α 1;
  - the light state fills on the ink register (FL-6).

  The rest does not fit:
  - C′ adds an attribute, `@utility glass-ink`, new exports (`glassGroundTone`, `publishGlassField` and their helpers) and a rebound `.published-roster` (Cp-proto §2), against G5's empty roster diff. Its per-plate `--muted-foreground` is a behaviour break with MIGRATION rows. That is 11.0.0 or W-H material.
  - The dark arm (lift to L 0.278-0.295, perimeter 0.60) violates G6, "dark untouched within ΔE 0.002". It is W-H.2's.
  - The sampler deletion (FL-1) and `backdropMode` (FL-16) are removals, for W-H.1.
- **Three things nobody has measured, and W-0.1 needs each.**
  1. **The minor split itself.** Every pass-2 number has the sampler deleted. The 10.2.0 subset (10.1.0's sampler, clamp and knee still present, plus only the light-arm values) was never built or measured. Its clamp may move the light veil off the bound.
  2. **O-66 §1.** W-0.1's G2 (trigger field ΔE ≤ 0.01) and G3 (the highlighted row not grey) were ordered as a D3 pass-2 witness (`FORMATION-PROGRESS.md:34`). No pass-2 seat read them.
  3. **O-62 off paper.** W-0.1's G1 is M-1, paper-only. The floor's light arm passes G1 and still reads `#e2dcd7` over black. G1 green would be a vacuous green against the owner's frame.

### P2-6.4 · What would end the loop

- Floor v2 converges once Q-1 and Q-11 are ruled and N-1..N-4 are built. It is then the 10.2.0 light arm's source (P2-4), after two consecutive clean passes.
- C″ converges or banks on C-7. If it converges, it is an 11.0.0 candidate (it adds names and changes `--muted-foreground` inside plates) and never 10.2.0's.
- A winner between them is named only after both are measured under H3. That cannot happen before pass 3 closes, and the 10.2.0 tag waits for pass 4.

## P3-0 · In brief

| route | status | rows (critic's count) | clean? | reason in one line |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| **floor v2** (the null route, A′ merged) | **ADVANCE**, the one advancing route | 34/44 = 0.77 | **no** | every gated witness green bar five rows of its own and five that wait on the owner. Its own five are now ruled here (P3-4.2) |
| **C″** · floor v2 + a resolver-checked claim | **BANK** (a component) | 35/48 = 0.73 | **no** | C-7 fails by structure: claimed light c1 wash T_c1 0.121 → 0.211, ×1.74 against ×2, and the ratio P/(P − g) = 1.733 does not depend on the ink. The row's own text banks it |
| D3-G | BLOCK, unchanged | — | — | Q-13 unruled; no engine ships a blend through an isolated group |
| D3-BF (B + F), D3-D | BANK, unchanged | — | — | no re-trigger fired in pass 3 |
| D3-E | RETIRE, unchanged | — | — | — |

**Pass 3 is not clean for any route.** So it is not the first of W-0.1's two consecutive clean passes. Pass 4 can be the first only if the owner rules Q-1 and Q-11 (with Q-3 for the gestalt row) before pass 4's critic runs, **and** the five floor-owned rows close in the build this registry orders (P3-5). The 10.2.0 veil recut then lands no earlier than the close of pass 5.

Three findings shape pass 4.

1. **Ground knowledge buys little transmission over the default field, and the ink buys the rest.** Pass 2 credited C′ with light c1 wash T_c1 0.723 against the floor's 0.121. Pass 3 split it: a claim at the floor's own ink reaches 0.211, and the ink lever (Q-12) supplies the remaining ×3.4 (Cpp-spec §2.3, Cpp-proto §4). Over a kinder field the claim pays: the demo's story Aurora at ceiling 0.6 computes content bound 0.494 light, T ≈ 0.51 (Cpp-proto §4). So any thinner glass over the default aurora now rests on the owner's Q-12, not on a declaration.
2. **Floor v2 is the material, and its remaining reds are small, named and buildable.** The dark capsule's `-strong` ink sits 0.013 short of M-4's 0.08. The dark focus veil does not dim (+0.006). The Select trigger and the Input paint two surfaces (O-66 G2). The highlighted menu row darkens with no tint (O-66 G3). And the inherited modal stack and dock glow break O-87. Each is a token value or one declaration (P3-4.2).
3. **The light veil off paper is unchanged by every pass-3 build.** Floor v2, the minor split and C″ each read **48 of 66** off-paper light composites grey: over black `#e2dcd7` (L 0.898, C 0.0095), over the story's deep canvas `#e3e3e3` (C 0.0000) (FV2 §4.3, MS §2.2, Cpp-proto §3). No seat can change this without a ruling: Q-11 is the owner's, and so are three rows of every route.

## P3-1 · Per route

### P3-1.1 · Floor v2 · ADVANCE, 0.77 (34/44)

**Proved by running.** FV2 §4, run twice in full (every verdict reproduced row for row; STACK moved only with the §2 cure), and re-run unclaimed by Cpp-crit §1 (= FV2 §4.2).
- Chromium, both themes: RAW 48, M-1 11, M-2 938, M-2m 430 (the 100 s drift included, 43/43 inks ≥ 4.5 in every frame), M-3 354, M-5 20, M-7 8, M-8 8, STACK 24, W-A 216, W-S 24 all PASS; T reported (light 0.110 / 0.075 / 0.035, dark 0.125 / 0.086 / 0.043, T_c1 ≤ 0.144).
- Toolchain: vue-tsc exit 0 on both projects; units 2318 passed, 0 failed; library build exit 0; verifier CLEAN bar the owed ratchet rebind (−18,803 B, 2,900,254 B) (FV2 §7).
- **One defect of FL-14 found and cured** (FV2 §2): a held plate stands down its `backdrop-filter`, so its carriers at `z-index: −1` escaped the host (STACK 12/24, wash-held fg 1.20 over an opaque ancestor). `isolation: isolate` in `@utility glass-plate` cures it; `plant-noisolation` reds it again.

**Why it banks.** C-7 is the route's licence to exist, and it is arithmetic: T_claim / T_floor = P/(P − g), with P 0.9736 and the default c1's claimed light tone g 0.4117. Two times needs g ≥ 0.487. No seat can move the default field, and the ink lever is the owner's Q-12.

**The banked component** (the claim, the resolver, the publication; `D3-Cpp-proto.patch`). Its entry in the bank:

| field | value |
|---|---|
| re-trigger | **any one of:** (a) the owner rules Q-12 (b), a darker static muted ink, applied to the floor as well; (b) the owner rules Q-11 (c), chrome thins only where a claim knows the ground; (c) the aurora wave lowers the default ceiling so g ≥ 0.487 (c0.5 computes T ×3.5, Cpp-spec risk 2) |
| owed before re-entry | **C-4:** a follow mechanism that sees every cascade path. The relative-colour read P2-6.3 named, or a refusal on any change the resolver cannot observe; a `MutationObserver` on two nodes is neither. **FL-7 and row 20, decided here:** the held floor of a chrome rung is `--glass-bound`, under a claim or not (one declaration on the chrome rungs). Q-3 (a) is the default, FL-7's text says so, and the three prose lines are then true. **Instrument:** the two-ground α estimator is invalid under per-stand claims (Cpp-proto §7.1), and W-S cannot see a lying stamp on an inert claim (§7.2). The unit arm carries the truth leg until W-S does |
| what it already proves | C-1 and C-2 with no ink solve; the stamp contract (`data-glass-ground-state`); the per-host worst-tones publication (C-6); Q-8 (c) on the panel spring (`lagonly` red) |

## P3-5 · Pass-4 plan

### P3-5.1 · Routes

| route | centre | judged on |
|---|---|---|
| **floor v3** = floor v2 + R-1..R-5, plus a variant per open owner option | the bound, static ink, FL-1..FL-16 | the same 44 rows (24 core + FL-1..FL-16 + N-1..N-4). No row is added, struck or renamed |
| **the minor split v2** (a build, not a route) | 10.1.0 + floor v2's light arm + R-3, R-4 in minor names + light perimeter 0.52 + FL-10 + FL-7's held value + W-0.11's blur and census values | W-0.1 G1-G7, M-2, M-3, M-2h, H3-1 |
| C″ | BANK | not run unless a re-trigger in P3-1.2 fires |
| D3-G | BLOCK | not run unless Q-13 is ruled (b) or an engine ships the primitive |

### P3-5.2 · Order (browser seats serialized, as every pass)

1. **H4, the instrument fixes I-1..I-4** (P3-2), each with a fixture PASS and a planted FAIL in both engines, then floor v2 re-run under H4 as the baseline. No witness changes its gate except where an owner ruling has arrived (I-2 follows Q-11's text).
2. **Floor v3.** Build R-1..R-5 on `floor-v2.patch`. Run the full battery once, both engines, with every R plant. Build each open owner option as a measured injection over the same build, so a ruling closes its rows by selection, not by another pass:
   - Q-1 (c): the step arithmetic at `glass.css:273-285` deleted, three materials (content, chrome, room); M-6 read under (c)'s text.
   - Q-11 (b): light chrome opaque; M-1x read under `--q11 b`. Report which content rungs stay grey off paper.
3. **The minor split v2.** Build and measure once under H4 against G1-G7, M-2, M-3 (light green on every row is the aim, including the wash well over black at 2.96), M-2h (held dock ≥ 4.5), and H3-1. Also measure one `.dark` island cell against HEAD (the unmeasured side effect in MS §1.1), and rewrite the 36 pinned test arms to the light law in the build, so the landing debt is measured too.
4. **The critic**, one seat, adversarial, on floor v3 and the minor v2, scoring the 44 rows from its own re-run. It may not add rows; a new defect class goes to the pass-5 instrument list.
5. **Agglomeration**, reading any owner rulings that have arrived.

### P3-5.3 · What would make pass 4 clean

Floor v3 is clean at **0 open rows of 44**:
- rows 7, 12, 15, 16, 22 close on R-1..R-5 with their plants red (seat-closable);
- rows 3, 23 and N-2 close only when the owner rules Q-11 (and Q-3) and the build reads green under that ruling;
- rows 9 and N-1 close only when the owner rules Q-1 and the matching variant reads green.

If the owner has not ruled Q-1 and Q-11 by pass 4's critic, pass 4 cannot be clean, whatever the build reads. Those five rows are the whole of D3's critical path to 10.2.0.

**W-0.1's entry gate after pass 3.** Two consecutive clean passes are owed, and pass 3 was not one. Earliest landing: the close of pass 5, if passes 4 and 5 both close clean. W-0.1 then ships from the minor split v2, never from floor v3 (the removals, the dark arm and FL-14's `isolation` are W-H's), and only once its G1-G7 are green with Q-11 applied to G1's off-paper reading. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88) and on W-0.11 (O-87), whose token values R-5 and the blur ladder supply.

### P3-5.4 · What would end the loop

- **Floor v3 converges** once Q-1 and Q-11 are ruled and built, after two consecutive clean passes. It is then D3's converged specification: W-0.1's light arm (through the minor split v2) in 10.2.0, and the rest (FL-1, FL-16, the dark arm, FL-14) in the W-H landing.
- **C″ stays banked** unless a re-trigger fires. If one does, it re-enters as a route on the same 48 rows, with C-4 and the chrome held floor owed first.
- No other route is open. If the owner rules against the floor's centre (Q-3 (c), all chrome transmits), every banked family's re-trigger is re-read, and pass 5 opens whichever fires.

## P4-6 · Pass-5 plan

### P4-6.1 · Routes

| route | centre | judged on |
|---|---|---|
| **floor v4** = floor v3 + R-6 (+ R-8's ladder if it moves) + whatever the owner has ruled, selected from the variants | the bound, static ink, FL-1..FL-16 | **the same 44 rows** (24 core + FL-1..FL-16 + N-1..N-4). No row is added, struck or renamed |
| **the minor split v3** (a build, not a route) | minor v2 + R-6 (minor names) + R-7 (0.54) + R-8's ladder + the demo copy; FL-14 as an injected variant | W-0.1 G1-G7 (G6 read per R-9, raw beside it), M-2, M-3, M-2h, H3-1 off paper under whatever Q-11 text has arrived; and W-0.11 G1-G6 on the same tree, since both land in one band |
| C″ | BANK | not run unless a re-trigger fires (Q-11 content clause "not" fires (c)) |
| D3-G | BLOCK | not run unless Q-13 (b) or an engine ships the primitive |

### P4-6.2 · Order (browser seats serialized; every WebKit run under `guarded --exclusive webkit`)

1. **H5, the instrument fixes H5-1..H5-5**, each with a fixture PASS and a planted FAIL in both engines. Then floor v3 re-runs under H5 as the baseline. No witness changes its gate except where an owner ruling has arrived (H5-4 follows Q-11's text).
2. **Floor v4.** Build R-6 on `floor-v3.patch`, and confirm the `[data-highlighted]` coverage of every `.glass-menu-row` composer before the build. Run the full battery once, both engines, with R-6's plant. Select any ruled owner option from the pass-4 variants (`fv3/q1c.*`, `fv3/q11b.*`); build a ruling only if it names something no variant carries. If R-8 moves the ladder (step 3 runs its search first, or a shared seat does), floor v4 takes it and re-reads M-2, M-8 and T.
3. **The minor split v3.** Build R-6, R-7, R-8 (the search) and the demo copy. Measure once under H5 against G1-G7, with G6 read per R-9 and the raw value beside it. Also measure M-2, M-3 (light green on every resting row is the aim), M-2h, H3-1, and W-0.11 G1-G6. Add the FL-14 variant. Re-read the `.dark` island only if R-7 or R-8 moves a dark composite.
4. **The critic**, one seat, adversarial, on floor v4 and the minor v3. It re-captures the minor's perimeter and radius values itself (pass 4's critic took the 0.54 and 0.56 numbers from the seat), and it judges the edge gleam and H5-1's frames under O-87. It may not add rows. A new defect class goes to the pass-6 instrument list.
5. **Agglomeration**, reading any owner rulings that have arrived.

### P4-6.3 · What would make pass 5 clean

Floor v4 is clean at **0 open rows of 44**:
- rows 20 and FL-12 close on R-6, with R-4-as-built red under H5-3 and the prose sweep (seat-closable);
- rows 9 and N-1 close when the owner rules Q-1. Under (a) they close by selection (88/88 is already measured); under (c) they need the room ruling and value;
- rows 3 and N-2 close when the owner rules Q-11's chrome clause. Under (a) by its text; under (b) by `var-q11b` with H5-4;
- row 23 closes when the owner rules Q-11's content clause and Q-3 and accepts the gestalt frames (including H5-1's bruise frame).

If the owner has not ruled Q-1 and Q-11 by pass 5's critic, pass 5 cannot be clean, whatever the build reads. Those five rows remain D3's whole critical path to 10.2.0. Each pass that closes unruled moves the earliest landing out by one pass.

**W-0.1's entry gate after pass 4.** 0 of 2 clean passes. Earliest landing: the close of pass 6, if passes 5 and 6 are both clean. W-0.1 ships from the minor split v3, never from floor v4 (the removals, the dark arm and, unless the variant earns it, FL-14 are W-H's). It ships only once G1-G7 are green (G6 per R-9) with Q-11's ruling applied to G1's off-paper reading. W-0.11 ships from the same tree with its G1-G6 green, R-8's ladder included. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88).

### P4-6.4 · What would end the loop

- **Floor v4 converges** once Q-1 and Q-11 (both clauses) are ruled and selected or built, after two consecutive clean passes. It is then D3's converged specification: W-0.1's light arm and W-0.11's tokens (through the minor split v3) in 10.2.0, and the rest (FL-1, FL-16, the dark arm, FL-14 unless the variant moves it, R-1, R-2, R-3's `--control-surface-blur` removal) in the W-H landing.
- **C″ stays banked** unless a re-trigger fires. The live one is Q-11's content clause ruled "not accepted", which is the (c) re-trigger. It would re-enter on the same 48 rows, with C-4 and the chrome held floor owed first.
- If the owner rules against the floor's centre (Q-3 (c), all chrome transmits), every banked family's re-trigger is re-read, and pass 6 opens whichever fires.

**Pass 5 is not clean** (7 rows open; clean means 0). **The two-clean count stays at 0 of 2.** Pass 6 can be the first clean pass only if the owner rules Q-1, Q-3 and Q-11 (both clauses) and looks at the frames before pass 6's critic runs, **and** R-10 and R-11 close in the build (P5-2.2). Four findings shape pass 6.

1. **Floor v4's own work is done except prose and one ladder.** Every route-owned value the registry has ruled since pass 3 now reads green in paint with its plant red: R-1..R-6 on the floor, R-6..R-9 on the minor. What is left of the route is six comments (R-10) and taking the minor's blur ladder (R-11), which moves one attributed halo reading. Neither changes a register or a name.
2. **The loop is at an owner-gated fixed point.** Five of the seven open rows (3, 9, 23, N-1, N-2) have read the same since pass 3, under every build, and each closes by selection under the default with no build: M-6 `--q1 a` 88/88 in both engines, M-1x and W-A under (a)'s text, and under (b) `var-q11b` now reads green on H5-4's chrome leg in both engines (Crit §1). No seat can close them. A pass that runs unruled can at best reach 39/44.
3. **A deleted mechanism's prose outlives it.** The prose sweep has read only the sentences a ruling named. Two seats and one critic in pass 4, and both pass-5 seats, swept past the earned-darken sentences (Crit A-1). H6-1 puts the deleted mechanism's names under a gated static check over the whole tree, not just the touched files.
4. **The dark focus veil reads as a smudge on bright grounds, and no uniform dim avoids it.** R-2's 40 % black dims the dark page by ΔL −0.022 and the aurora's focus spot by −0.199, 8.9× as much (H5 §3.3, Crit A-3). The critic reads the frame as "not deft" under O-87. A multiplicative dim that reaches −0.02 on a page at L ≈ 0.15 darkens a ground at L ≈ 0.66 several times more in L. That is this seat's arithmetic, not a measurement, but it means no value of R-2's kind fixes the frame. It goes to the owner as Q-14 (P5-4).

### P5-3.4 · Can W-0.1 and W-0.11 ship from the minor v3 in 10.2.0? No

1. **The entry gate:** 0 of 2 clean passes (W-0.1 §3, W-0.11 §3.3). With the owner unruled, no pass can be clean.
2. **Q-11's content clause:** G1 off paper reads 48/66 grey with content included, and only the owner can say whether 10.2.0 may ship grey content off paper.
3. **FL-14 in source:** ruled in (P5-3.2), measured only as an injection. The minor v4 builds it.
4. **R-9 in W-0.1's text:** G6 is green only under R-9's reading. W-0.1 §5 G6 must take it when W-0.1 and W-0.11 land together, or G6 is red on the dark dock's cast alone (0.0038). This seat cannot edit the wave. The edit is owed to the landing, and the owner may override.
5. **Two probe names in the waves do not exist in the tree.** W-0.1 §5 reads G2 and G3 with `scripts/probes/band-0/measure.mjs`, and W-0.11 §5 reads its census at `scripts/probes/band-0/lighting-census.mjs`. D3 reads them with `harness/o66-field-row.mjs` and `pass-4/lighting-census.mjs`, W-0.11 appendix A verbatim (Crit §9). W-0.3 and W-0.11 land those probes. The landing binds each gate to one probe (P-3) and re-reads it there.

W-0.11 has no blocker of its own: its G1-G6 are green on the minor v3, R-8's ladder included. It shares W-0.1's entry gate, because both take D3's converged specification and the two land in one band. Earliest landing for both: **the close of pass 7**, if passes 6 and 7 are both clean. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88).

## P5-6 · Pass-6 plan

### P5-6.1 · Routes

### P5-6.2 · Order (browser seats serialized; every WebKit run under `guarded --exclusive webkit`)

1. **H6.** H6-0 (the Chromium arguments) and H6-1 (the deleted-mechanism check), each with a fixture PASS and a planted FAIL. Then the owed rows: the GLANCE WebKit self-test row and the critic's two WebKit re-captures. Then floor v4 re-runs under H6 in both engines as the baseline, with a `diff` against pass 5's baseline. No witness changes its gate except where an owner ruling has arrived.
2. **Floor v5.** Build R-10, R-11 and R-12 on `floor-v4.patch`. Run the full battery once in both engines with each plant: the H6-1 grep, the ladder `diff`, and the demo probe. Read `diag-held-noveil` at 18 px in both themes for N-3. Select any ruled owner option from the variants (`fv3/q1c.*`, `fv3/q11b.*`), and build only what no variant carries.
3. **The minor split v4.** Build FL-14 in source, R-12 and R-13. Measure once under H6 against G1-G7 (G6 per R-9 and raw), W-0.11 G1-G6, M-2, M-3, M-2h, H3-1 and STACK, in both engines, with `plant-noisolation`. Re-read the `.dark` island only if FL-14 moves a dark composite.
4. **The critic**, one seat, adversarial, on floor v5 and the minor v4. It runs the demo probe for R-12 itself and judges the frames under O-87. It may not add rows. A new defect class goes to the pass-7 instrument list.
5. **Agglomeration**, reading any owner rulings that have arrived.

**On the fixed point.** After R-10..R-13 the route owns no open row. Three passes have run with the owner's five rows unchanged. So the ruling sheet in P5-4 (one message, five answers) is worth more than another unruled battery. The driver may hold step 4 until the rulings arrive once steps 1-3 have banked. A pass whose critic runs unruled cannot be clean, and each such pass moves the earliest landing out by one more pass.

### P5-6.3 · What would make pass 6 clean

Floor v5 is clean at **0 open rows of 44**:
- rows 20 and FL-12 close on R-10, with floor v4's six lines red under H6-1 (seat-closable);
- R-11 holds N-3 as PASS (the dark half attributed, or a held-floor value ruled from measurement), with every other row unmoved by the ladder;
- rows 9 and N-1 close when the owner rules Q-1. Under (a) they close by selection (88/88 measured);
- rows 3 and N-2 close when the owner rules Q-11's chrome clause, by (a)'s text or by selecting `fv3/q11b.*` (green under H5-4);
- row 23 closes when the owner rules Q-11's content clause, Q-3 and Q-14 and accepts the frames.

If the owner has not ruled Q-1, Q-3, Q-11 (both clauses) and Q-14 by pass 6's critic, pass 6 cannot be clean, whatever the builds read.

**W-0.1's and W-0.11's entry gate after pass 5:** 0 of 2 clean passes. Earliest landing: the close of **pass 7**, if passes 6 and 7 are both clean. Both ship from the minor split (v4 or later), never from the floor: the removals, the dark arm and `--control-surface-blur` are W-H's (P5-3.3). They ship only once G1-G7 are green (G6 per R-9, in W-0.1's text), with Q-11's ruling applied to G1's off-paper reading, FL-14 built in source, and each gate bound to one probe at landing. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88).

### P5-6.4 · What would end the loop

- **Floor v5 converges** once Q-1, Q-3, Q-11 (both clauses) and Q-14 are ruled and selected or built, after two consecutive clean passes. It is then D3's converged specification: W-0.1's light arm and W-0.11's tokens in 10.2.0, through the minor split and its table (P5-3.3), and the rest in the W-H landing.
- **C″ stays banked** unless a re-trigger fires. The live one is Q-11's content clause ruled "not accepted", which is re-trigger (c). It would re-enter on the same 48 rows, with C-4 and the chrome held floor owed first.

### P6-6.2 · Order (browser seats serialized; every WebKit run under `guarded --exclusive webkit`, queued only after the seat's Chromium phases)

0. **The ruling sheet goes to the owner now** (P6-4). It costs one message, and every pass without an answer moves the landing out by one.
1. **H7.** H7-1, H7-2 and H7-3, each with its fixture PASS and planted FAIL. H7-1 re-scores the banked M-3 PNGs of floor v5, the minor v4 and HEAD with no new capture, and prints a `diff` of every M-3 verdict against H6's. Only three-column fallback rings may move.
2. **Floor v6.** Build R-15 on `floor-v5.patch`, with the declaration built from the patch's removed lines (R-15 (iii)). Prove the specimen CSS and dist-demo outputs byte-identical to floor v5's. If they are, the verdicts carry, and the seat runs the static checks (DEL under H7-2, LAW, census, HOV, the ladder `diff`), the toolchain and H7-1's M-3 re-read. If they are not, it runs the full battery in both engines. Select any ruled owner option from the variants (`pass-4/fv3/q1c.*`, `fv3/q11b.*`), and build only what no variant carries. A Q-14 (b) ruling is built as R-2's deletion in the dark arm and re-reads row 12.
3. **The minor split v5.** Build R-16. Prove the byte identity as in step 2, then run H7-3, H7-1's M-3 re-read with G7's M-3 half, and the toolchain. Record R-16 (iii)'s sentence-by-sentence verdicts.
4. **The critic**, one seat, adversarial, on floor v6 and the minor v5, **held until the owner's answers arrive**, unless the driver records why it runs unruled. It re-reads FL-14's dark-composite movement (P6-5), checks R-14's reader against the raw PNGs and its plants, and checks R-15's declaration against the patch's removed lines. It may not add rows. A new defect class goes to the pass-8 instrument list.
5. **Agglomeration**, reading any owner rulings that have arrived.

### P7-6.2 · Order (browser seats serialized; every WebKit run under `guarded --exclusive webkit`, captures split from witnesses)

0. **The ruling sheet goes to the owner now** (P7-4). It is one message. Every pass that closes without an answer moves the landing out by one.
1. **H8.** H8-1 (three arms) and H8-2, each with its fixture PASS and planted FAIL in the self-test. Static only, `--light`.
2. **Floor v7.** Build R-17 on `floor-v6.patch`. Prove the specimen dist byte-identical to floor v6's and the dist-demo byte-identical to floor v6's as built (R-17 touches only `tests/`). If both hold, run the static checks (DEL under H8-1 and H8-2, LAW, census, HOV, the ladder `diff`) and the toolchain with vitest, and no browser. Select any ruled owner option from the variants (`pass-4/fv3/q1c.*`, `fv3/q11b.*`), which then requires the full battery; build only what no variant carries. A Q-14 (b) ruling is built as R-2's deletion in the dark arm and re-reads row 12.
3. **The minor split v6.** Build R-18. Prove byte identity to the minor v5, then run H7-3 with the new declaration, H8-1 over its own patch, and the toolchain. No browser if the bytes hold.
4. **The owed WebKit cells** (P7-5), one seat, queued only after steps 2-3. Each capture group is its own `guarded --exclusive webkit --timeout 60` admission (plants; Close rings plus the checker red's two re-runs; the dark drift window twice). Witnesses run `--light` outside the lock. A cell that still cannot get the lock in 90 min is recorded as owed again and does not hold the pass.
5. **The critic**, one seat, adversarial, on floor v7 and the minor v6, **held until the owner's answers arrive**. Every pass-8 build is browser-free, so holding it costs no machine time. If the driver runs it unruled, it records why, and the pass cannot be clean. The critic checks R-17's two arms against the patch (each `exact` entry read in context), the byte-identity proofs, and R-18's sentences against A-3's table. It may not add rows, and a new defect class goes to the pass-9 instrument list.
6. **Agglomeration**, reading any owner rulings that have arrived.

### P7-6.3 · What would make pass 8 clean

Floor v7 is clean at **0 open rows of 44**:
- rows 20 and FL-12 close on R-17: H8-1 at 0 over every touched file with its value arm, floor v6 red under it, and the critic finding no sentence in a touched file that describes a deleted or changed mechanism of the patch as live;
- rows 9 and N-1 close when the owner rules Q-1, by selection under (a);
- rows 3 and N-2 close when the owner rules Q-11's chrome clause, by (a)'s text or by selecting `fv3/q11b.*`;
- row 23 closes when the owner rules Q-11's content clause, Q-3 and Q-14 and accepts the frames.

If the owner has not ruled by pass 8's critic, pass 8 cannot be clean, whatever the builds read.

**W-0.1's and W-0.11's entry gate after pass 7:** 0 of 2 clean passes. Earliest landing: the close of **pass 9**, if passes 8 and 9 are both clean. Both ship from the minor split (v6 or later), never from the floor (P5-3.3), once G1-G7 are green (G6 per R-9, in W-0.1's text), Q-11's ruling is applied to G1's off-paper reading, R-18 is built, each gate is bound to one probe, and the ratchet is rebound at landing. 10.2.0 is also gated on D2 converging (O-88).

### P7-6.4 · What would end the loop

- **Floor v7 converges** once Q-1, Q-3, Q-11 (both clauses) and Q-14 are ruled and selected or built, R-17 closes rows 20 and FL-12, and two consecutive passes are clean. It is then D3's converged specification: W-0.1's light arm and W-0.11's tokens through the minor split and its table (P5-3.3 as amended by P6-3.2 and P7-3.2) in 10.2.0, and the rest in the W-H landing.
- **The fixed point.** If pass 8 closes rows 20 and FL-12 and the sheet is still unanswered, floor v7 reads 39/44 with only rows 3, 9, 23, N-1 and N-2 open. No route work can move those rows, so that is the loop's owner-gated fixed point. In that case **pass 9 is launched only when the owner's answers arrive**. A further pass without them re-measures the same tree and cannot be clean.
- **C″ stays banked** unless a re-trigger fires. The live one is Q-11's content clause ruled "not accepted" (re-trigger (c)). It would re-enter on its 48 rows, with C-4 and the chrome held floor owed first.
- If the owner rules Q-3 (c), every banked family's re-trigger is re-read, and the next pass opens whichever fires.
- If the owner overrides R-14, row 6 returns to FV5 §5's options, and the registry rules among them. (i)'s 0.60 would move R-7 and O-98 R-3's headroom with it.

Four findings shape pass 9.

4. **The owner-gated rows are six passes old.** Rows 3, 9, 23, N-1 and N-2 have read the same since pass 3, and the sheet (P8-4) is unchanged because no specimen pixel has moved since pass 7. Pass 9 can reach the fixed point without the owner. It cannot be clean without the owner.
